#2089. Wolves should challenge Birmingham City and Middlesbrough for perfect Andre partner: Opinion

Wolves need to be keeping their options open for the January window despite a decent start to the season. Cesar Peixoto’s side currently sit 4th in the Championship and a good window will put them in a very strong position to push for automatic promotion.

Wolves should be keeping a close eye on Arthur Avom ahead of the January transfer window, with the Lorient midfielder offering an opportunity to strengthen their promotion push while potentially laying the foundations for a Premier League midfield.

The 21-year-old has emerged as an attractive target for several English clubs, with Birmingham City and Middlesbrough reportedly tracking his situation. However, Wolves should be looking to get ahead of their Championship rivals, particularly given the opportunity to negotiate a relatively affordable deal.

Avom has already established himself in French football, making 31 Ligue 1 appearances last season after helping Lorient win promotion from the second tier in 2024/25. He has continued to feature regularly this term and has also earned senior international recognition with Cameroon.

His contract situation makes the opportunity even more interesting. Lorient’s agreement with Avom currently runs until June 2027, potentially leaving the French club with limited leverage next summer.

The biggest attraction is how well Avom could complement Andre. Wolves already have an established midfield anchor in the Brazilian, who committed his future to the club until 2030 following relegation from the Premier League.

Andre provides defensive discipline, composure and the ability to dictate possession, whereas Avom offers greater mobility, energy and forward-running ability. The Cameroonian is comfortable winning possession, carrying the ball through midfield and contributing defensively, making him a natural candidate to operate alongside Andre in a double pivot.

That partnership could be particularly valuable across a demanding 46-game Championship campaign, allowing Andre to concentrate on controlling games while Avom supplies the athleticism and intensity needed to compete in both boxes.

There is also a longer-term argument for the move. Much of Wolves’ existing midfield group is already established, with Marshall Munetsi approaching 30 and Tommy Doyle and Boubacar Traore in their mid-twenties.

Avom would introduce another young option alongside Jordan James, helping reduce the need for an extensive midfield rebuild if promotion is secured. His experience in Ligue 1 and international football also means he would not necessarily need several seasons to adapt to a higher level.

Wolves cannot afford to overlook the immediate competition, either. Birmingham and Middlesbrough are both capable of offering attractive projects, while previous Premier League interest demonstrates Avom’s growing reputation.

If Wolves are serious about building a team capable of returning to the top flight and staying there, Avom is exactly the type of recruitment they should be considering. A January move could strengthen their promotion ambitions while giving them a potentially long-term partner for Andre.

#2086. Gomes scores for Aston Villa in defeat by Bayern Munich in Hong Kong

Aston Villa’s tour of the Far East ended in defeat as they lost 2-1 to German champions Bayern Munich in a pre-season friendly at the Kai Tak Sports Park in Hong Kong on Friday.

Bayern took the lead inside the final 10 minutes of the first half, with defender Kim Min-jae heading in from Tom Bischof’s sublime free-kick. Villa goalkeeper Marco Bizot made a string of saves in the second half to keep Bayern’s lead at one goal until former Liverpool forward Luis Díaz smashed in Bayern’s second off the underside of the bar.

Just as the game appeared to be out of reach for Aston Villa, who had struggled to create much going forward for most of the game, Unai Emery’s side showed some life late on. Summer signing João Gomes’s goal from the edge of the box gave them hope before Tammy Abraham nearly brought them back onto level terms, only to be denied by a fine save from 40-year-old Manuel Neuer.

Aston Villa are next in action in six days’ time when they take on Paris St-Germain in the Uefa Super Cup before kicking off their Premier League campaign at Brighton. Bayern have one more friendly to come against RB Leipzig before the DFL-Supercup against Borussia Dortmund. They then kick off the defence of their Bundesliga crown against Stuttgart at home on August 28.

Stepping up in the big moments! 🐐🧤 pic.twitter.com/r14rF39XpR

Unai Emery’s men head home from the Far East to face PSG in the Uefa Super Cup in Salzburg next week and then have one more pre-season friendly against Borussia Monchengladbach before starting their Premier League campaign at Brighton.

Possession: Aston Villa 44%-56% Bayern MunichShots: 8-16Shots on target: 3-8Corners: 3-7

Bizot nearly has a calamitous moment coming out of his penalty area but gets away with it.

The final whistle is blown soon after and Bayern hold on to beat Villa 2-1 in Hong Kong. They had looked comfortable when Diaz put them 2-0 up but Villa kept going until the end.

Victory in the final match of the #AudiFCBTour! 💪🔥 pic.twitter.com/UFhQhme62i

The space opens up for Palhinha to shoot from range but Bizot makes a fairly comfortable save down to his right.

It was all Bayern for 80 minutes of this game but Villa have come to life in the closing stages, with Vincent Kompany’s side now holding on a bit.

When Diaz scored Bayern’s second goal, Aston Villa were out of this game but it has been all Villa since then. They look the more likely to get a goal.

Gomes’ free-kick out on the left is parried behind by Neuer for a Villa corner on the right. The corner comes to nothing.

For 80 minutes, Aston Villa barely looked like scoring but come so close to getting two goals in just a few minutes. Abraham meets a cross from the right and must think he has scored but his header is brilliantly saved by the 40-year-old Neuer.

Maybe this game is not quite done and dusted yet. A cross from the right picks out Gomes on the edge of the box. The Brazilian takes one touch before toe poking past Neuer and into the Bayern net. Gomes meant that finish and Villa have some hope.

Abraham should have done better there. He has the space to drive down Villa’s right and has a decision to make; cross the ball to his left and drive forward himself to shoot. He takes a while to make the decision before his attempted cross is intercepted by Ito.

Boey’s yellow card was his final act of the game as he is replaced by Della Rovere. Boey did seem to be struggling with some cramp.

Bayern receive their second yellow card of the game as Boey is booked for cynically pulling down Lynch.

Cash, Maatsen and Buendia have gone off for Aston Villa.

Double substitution from Bayern as Pavic and Assomo replace Tah and Brown.

A fantastic goal to put the Germans 2-0 up and it is nothing more than they deserve. Ibrahimovic finds Diaz in space on the left and the Colombian drives at Cash. As he enters the Villa box, Diaz cuts inside onto his preferred right foot and smashes a shot towards goal. It appeared to go right over Bizot, who opts not to put a hand up. The ball crashes off the underside of the bar and goes in. It has been all Bayern in this second half and that goal had been coming.

Bayern are knocking on the door and come very close to doubling their lead. Diaz’s shot from the edge of the box looks destined to hit the net with Bizot beaten but fortunately for Villa Abraham is on hand on his own goal line to make the block.

Bizot makes another great save. Bayern win a free-kick out on the right after Brown was fouled. Bischoff opts to shoot instead of crossing and nearly beats Bizot at his near post but the Aston Villa keeper gets across to his left to make the save.

Unai Emery makes a double substitution as Mings and Lynch replace Torres and Guessand. Mings takes the captain’s armband.

That is a fantastic double save from Bizot, first denying Stanisic and then Ibrahimovic. Both of those shots came through bodies so Bizot did a good job of reacting and making the saves with his legs on both occasions.

It is safe to say the Bayern fans out in Hong Kong love Diaz, who is cheered every time he touches the ball.

Vincent Kompany is going to make a triple change as Ito, Diaz and Palhinha come on. Former Liverpool man Diaz received a great ovation from the fans in Hong Kong. Palhinha, who spent last season on loan at Tottenha, has been linked with a move to Aston Villa in this window.

Should Bayern have been awarded a penalty there? A ball is played in behind the Villa defence into the penalty area and Ibrahimovic appeared to be brought down but the referee waves those appeals away. On first viewing, that appeared to be a penalty.

Just before the hour mark, it is time for some changes from Aston Villa. Abraham and Bogarde come on for Madjo and Kamara. This is Abraham’s first appearance of this pre-season.

Good play from Gomes earns Aston Villa a free-kick not far outside the Bayern penalty area out on the left, in a similar position to the free-kick that Bayern scored from in the first half. Buendia clips in a good ball to the back post, where Guessand looks likely to score but instead misses the ball on the stretch. Should Guessand have done better with that? Probably.

Brown was starting in his customary left-back position for Bayern in the first half but is now operating out on the right wing.

Gomes leaves one late on Pavlovic and, had this been a competitive fixture, I suspect that might have been a yellow card.

Tah is brought down by Gomes and Bayern will have a free-kick out on the right not a million miles outside the Villa penalty area. The delivery though is poor and easily cleared away.

Bayern have brought Stanisic on at half-time for Laimer. Aston Villa have not made a change at the break.

Possession: Aston Villa 45%-55% Bayern MunichShots: 4-7Shots on target: 1-2Corners: 1-2

The first half in Hong Kong is brought to an end and Bayern take a 1-0 lead into the break courtesy of Kim’s goal.

There will be one added minute at the end of this first half.

Aston Villa have struggled to create much going forward and have not really had a genuine goal-scoring opportunity in this first half. Their forward line though will look quite different when they kick off the seaosn than it does today.

The German side take the lead in Hong Kong. Bischof’s left-footed delivery from a free-kick out on the left is sublime, whipping it right into the corridor of uncertainty. The quality of delivery means it is quite a simple header for Kim, who makes no mistake with his header into the far corner. Kim might have been offside but obviously there is no VAR in use today. 1-0 Bayern.

That is a waste in the end from Bayern. Boey fires a pass in-field into Laimer, who takes a fantastic first touch on the turn inside the Villa half. He drives through the Villa half and at the penalty area but his pass outside looking for Cardozo is poor and overhit, with the ball rolling out for a goal kick. Laimer will be annoyed at himself having done all the hard work and then failed to execute the easy part.

Bischof eyes one up from range and tries to pick out the top corner. Bizot is routed to his spot and is hoping the shot goes wide. Bischof’s effort is always curling wide, much to Bizot’s relief.

The first booking of the game goes the way of Bayern Munich. Guessand drives forward into the Bayern half and is brought down by a cynical foul from Cardozo. You were half expecting Cardozo to perhaps escape without a booking with it being a pre-season friendly but he does not. It was the right call to book him.

That was lovely from Pavlovic. The German midfielder shows good feet to beat Gomes before getting his shot away from a central position just outside the box. He forces Bizot into a decent save but one he should be making.

Bayern force Villa into an error and win themselves a corner as a result out on the left. It is delivered into a dangerous area at the front post but Villa do a good job of handling it and clearing their lines.

Bayern Munich have made two signings this summer; Brown from Bundesliga rivals Frankfurt and Saibari from PSV. Brown is starting at left-back for Bayern today with Saibari not involved as he closes in on a return from a knock picked up at the World Cup with Morocco. Brown feels like such a Bayern signing, nabbing a good player from a Bundesliga rival.

Aston Villa have made four signings so far this summer but just one is in their starting XI today in the form of Gomes, who joined from Midlands rivals Wolves this summer. Young Swiss midfielder Manzambi has joined from Freiburg but is currently out injured. Garnacho picked up a knock and Cisse, who has arrived from LASK, is not involved for Villa today.

The ball is played in behind the Aston Villa defence to pick out the run of Binder. On the edge of the Villa box, Binder gets the first touch but Lindelof is calm in defence and heads back to his keeper Bizot.

Moments later, Gomes is dispossessed inside his own half and Ibrahimovic has a shot from just outside the box but drags it wide of the far post.

Good work from 17-year-old Madjo down Aston Villa’s left wins them their first corner of the game. Buendia’s inswinger is headed away too easily at the near post. Aston Villa have seen their former set-piece coach Austin MacPhee nabbed this summer by Xabi Alonso’s Chelsea.

The referee is allowing the game to flow and thus not blowing for soft fouls.

The first chance of the game falls to Bayern Munich. Brown’s cross from the left is headed by Maatsen straight to Ibrahimovic on the edge of the box. Ibrahimovic, not any relation to Zlatan, takes the shot on first time but it is blocked by Maatsen, who makes up for that poor header with the block. The ball goes behind for a corner, which comes to nothing.

Interestingly, there are only eight outfield substitutions on the Aston Villa bench for this game, with the likes of Garnacho and McGinn picking up knocks the other day. It appears then that at least a couple of Villa outfield players will have to play the full 90.

Both sides emerge from the tunnel, Aston Villa led out by Matty Cash and Bayern Munich by Manuel Neuer, and we are closing in on kick-off in Hong Kong. A group of delegates shake the hands of both starting XIs.

Chelsea and Juventus played in this stadium the other night and it is safe to say that the pitch does not look in the best state ahead of kick-off today. It looks like it will continue to rip up and the last thing the two managers will want is to pick up any serious injuries.

Currently, before the two sides emerge, the crowd in Hong Kong are being treated to some pre-match dancing and entertainment, which includes people dressed as pandas and sharks dancing. Would the Premier League ever take inspiration and have dancing pandas before kick-off?

The likes of Ollie Watkins, Ezri Konsa and Emi Martinez are yet to return to training after their World Cup exploits and Aston Villa are also without captain John McGinn and new signing Alejandro Garnacho, who picked up knocks in their win over BG Pathum the other day.

Aston Villa have already sold Youri Tielemans and Morgan Rogers this summer, with Lucas Digne on the verge of a move to PSG, but could another starter be on the way out of Villa Park? England defender Ezri Konsa has been linked with moves to the likes of Arsenal and Liverpool but will Unai Emery be able to keep hold of the 28-year-old?

Aston Villa’s preparations for the new season continue out in Hong Kong as they take on Bundesliga champions Bayern Munich at the Kai Tak Stadium. These sides have met twice before; Aston Villa beat Bayern 1-0 to win the European Cup in 1981/82 before then meeting in the Champions League group stages during the 2024/25 season, with Jhon Duran’s long-range screamer giving Aston Villa a 1-0 win at Villa Park.

After losing two of their first three pre-season friendlies, Aston Villa have won their first two games on Asian soil and this game marks the end of their tour of the Far East. It has been a summer of significant change at Aston Villa thus far with some key members of their squad departing and some new faces arriving. Youri Tielemans has departed after Manchester United triggered his release clause and they then sold Morgan Rogers to Chelsea for a British transfer record £117m. Unai Emery’s side have brought in 20-year-old Swiss midfielder Johan Manzambi, Brazilian midfielder Joao Gomes and Alejandro Garnacho, with the deal for Garnacho being an initial loan. Manzambi is currently on the sidelines due to a knee injury.

Unai Emery’s side were dealt a blow during the World Cup as Belgian midfielder Amadou Onana picked up a serious knee injury. They were though boosted by the return of Boubacar Kamara after seven months out as he scored in the 3-1 friendly victory over BG Parthum. As one midfielder returned from injury in that game, another picked up a fresh injury as John McGinn suffered a knock to his knee. The likes of Ollie Watkins, Ezri Konsa and Emi Martinez are yet to return after their world Cup exploits. They begin their Premier League campaign with an away game on the south coast against Brighton on August 23 but will be involved in the Uefa Super Cup against PSG next week having lifted the Europa League last season.

Bayern Munich head into this season looking to win a third straight Bundesliga title and have won two of their three friendlies, losing once. They have added Moroccan attacker Ismael Saibari from PSV and German left-back Nathaniel Brown from Bundesliga rivals Eintracht Frankfurt to their ranks this summer. The likes of Harry Kane, Dayot Upamecano and Michael Olise are not available as they are still yet to return after World Cup duty but Vincent Kompany has confirmed that Bayern’s German stars are set to return.

After today, Bayern have one more friendly against RB Leipzig before the DFL-Supercup against Borussia Dortmund. They then kick off the defence of their Bundesliga crown against Stuttgart at home on August 28.

#2085. Journaling, no days off and 'Mamba Mentality': How Senne Lammens is trying to arrest his slide in form - and why his Man United team-mates must take the blame for his shattered confidence

Published: 21:00 EST, 9 November 2026 | Updated: 21:00 EST, 9 November 2026

Senne Lammens will have gone home on Sunday night, picked up his journal and tried to make sense of where it all went horribly wrong.

Journaling is what keeps Lammens’ mind at ease and what allows him to move on from moments of disappointment, of which Sunday afternoon against Aston Villa will have ranked high on his list.

Lammens may have lamented his decision to use his knees, rather than his hands, in attempting to deal with Alysson’s strike in the first minute. And that he parried a shot straight to Nicolas Jackson to make it 2-0 three minutes later. Lammens is big on accountability. Nobody will have needed to tell him that he could have done much better.

The responsibility for Villa goals three and four, however, lands squarely at the feet of his team-mates, a familiar pattern across the season so far.

Still, this was Lammens' worst performance in a Manchester United shirt, and the player himself is unlikely to have come to a different conclusion. But he has not become a bad goalkeeper overnight. Far from it.

While there was plenty of attention on his two costly mistakes against Villa, many have seemingly forgotten about a crucial one-on-one save Lammens made to deny Julio Enciso in the opening home match against Ipswich Town. It was 0-0 then and United would go on to win 5-2.

Senne Lammens has had a difficult start to the season - and was partly at fault for two of the goals Manchester United conceded in Sunday's 4-4 draw with Aston Villa

They have forgotten two brilliant saves he made at Fulham to deny Sander Berge, and then Josh King. The second, with his right foot, was sublime and kept a listing United in the game at 0-0.

Even in United's season opener, the 24-year-old did tremendously well to push Oli McBurnie’s goalbound effort onto the post, only for three of his back four to switch off, allowing Hull City to score en route to a 2-0 win.

Overlooked are the failed defensive assignments of Bryan Mbeumo (against Ipswich Town), or Carlos Baleba (against Leeds United), or the costly mistakes of Lisandro Martinez, Diogo Dalot and Leny Yoro.

Lammens’ own form has regressed, there’s no doubt about that, but no goalkeeper in the Premier League is being sold short by those in front of him more than the United No 1 right now.

Across 10 Premier League matches, no goalkeeper has faced more shots on his goal than Lammens (52), tied in first place with Newcastle United’s Lukas Hornicek.

It’s also worth noting that no goalkeeper has made more saves in a single Premier League match this season than Lammens’ eight at Chelsea. United went on to lose 2-0 that day.

Lammens has made the fourth-most saves this season and yet has conceded a league-high 19 goals (Crystal Palace's 24 have been let in by both Walter Benitez and Dean Henderson).

Last season, across 32 games, Lammens faced 118 shots, averaging 3.69 per game. Fast forward to this campaign and it’s 52 shots in 10 games, up to 5.2 per game, a stark increase in workload.

Only twice last season did Lammens face six or more shots in a Premier League game. This season it has happened four times already.

Lammens had a rush of the blood to the head when he came out – and missed out – as Evanilson knocked the ball past him to score Bournemouth’s third at Old Trafford last month

Mistakes, such as those at Everton, Fulham and at home to Villa, are costly. His distribution, too, has become a sore spot with Lammens ranking 15th of 16 ever-present goalkeepers for completed long passes (37 of 115 attempted).

But Lammens performed well in the last international break for Belgium behind competent defenders. He doesn’t have that luxury at United right now.

‘Sometimes in the end it comes down to maybe here and there, maybe a mistake from someone, but it doesn’t really start with that,’ Lammens said of United's defensive issues earlier this season.

‘I think it’s a team thing. The manager has been hammering a lot about defending as a team, being able to put pressure on the opposition team, especially on crosses.’

A key selling point for Lammens last season was the composure he brought and the calmness he exuded. He was everything to United that Andre Onana wasn’t. That’s been chipped away this season.

A nervousness has crept in. The misplaced pass against Tottenham Hotspur to Kobbie Mainoo that was never on, which saw him redeem himself with a strong save from Dominic Solanke. The rush of blood to come out – and miss out – as Evanilson knocked the ball past him to score Bournemouth’s third at Old Trafford. The judgment call to use his legs as the ball ricocheted off Martinez and into his own net at Fulham.

All of which has led to a pundit and social media pile-on of a goalkeeper that, in the eyes of some, is now out of his depth.

Which is where the journaling comes in. It helps Lammens' belief that things will turn for the better, and the composure of a man who didn’t get too high when he was anointed the Signing of the Season for 2025-26 across the entire Premier League, and who isn’t getting too low about the most scrutinised run of his career.

‘Especially at big clubs, you don't want a keeper who performs well one match and poorly the next,’ Lammens said back in September.

‘You want someone who is a rock behind the defence. Someone the team can rely on. That's definitely a major goal for me. It's something to embody as a goalkeeper.

‘You can compare it a bit to Edwin van der Sar. He's someone I admire greatly. What I want to take from his game is that calmness.’

Lammens' confidence was rocked by a mistake he made in Belgium's World Cup exit to Spain - and United's leaky defence has left him unable to build it back up

The 24-year-old is known to pass up days off to put in extra work, and sources say he is first in in the morning and last to head home at the end of training

Lammens is known to pass up days off to put in extra work, and sources say he is first in in the morning and last to head home at the end of training. There are even figures at United who believe parts of his game have improved on last season.

His confidence was rocked in the summer when his spill to Mikel Merino sent Belgium home from the World Cup and he has had little chance to build back that confidence given the storm back in Manchester.

Basketball legend Kobe Bryant is Lammens' idol. He sports the late LA Lakers star's numbers on his gloves and swears by his 'Mamba Mentality'. And if that mentality tells us anything, it’s that resilience and fearlessness win out.

Lammens is struggling but he’s still the same goalkeeper that was being lauded last season. He just needs a helping hand from those in front of him or this defence will be stuck in a never-ending cycle of misery.

#2084. West Ham should be keeping tabs on situation of out-of-favour Leeds United striker: Opinion

West Ham have a chance to propel themselves into title-winning contention in the January transfer window. WHU have been impressive to start the year and are the clear top scorers in the Championship, despite struggling to nail down a number nine.

West Ham United should be looking at Mateo Joseph as a potential January addition, with the Leeds United forward offering an interesting opportunity for Scott Parker as he looks to guide the Hammers back into the Premier League.

Parker has inherited a side with plenty of attacking quality, but the centre-forward position remains an area that could benefit from strengthening. With Joseph looking to be available in January, West Ham must be paying attention to his situation.

Joel Piroe has scored around four league goals since arriving on loan from Leeds, while Taty Castellanos has struggled to establish himself as a consistent goalscoring threat. West Ham are scoring freely, with 37 goals in their opening 15 Championship fixtures, but another option could prove important during the second half of the campaign.

Joseph would offer something different. The 23-year-old spent last season on loan at Mallorca, making 29 La Liga appearances, including 19 starts.

He registered two goals and three assists, with his underlying numbers suggesting there could be more to come from him in front of goal. His 4.92 expected goals compared with two actual strikes indicates that his finishing did not necessarily reflect the opportunities he found, according to FotMob.

Versatility is another attraction. Primarily a centre-forward or right winger, Joseph can operate across the frontline, giving Parker additional tactical flexibility. He also has experience of winning promotion from the Championship, having featured 39 times for Leeds during their successful 2024/25 campaign, recording three goals and three assists.

There is, however, an obvious complication. Joseph suffered an ACL injury while at Mallorca in April and has been recovering at Leeds. He is targeting a return to competitive action around January or February 2027, meaning any potential move would carry considerable fitness risk.

His modest senior goalscoring record also means West Ham would be taking a chance rather than signing an established Championship marksman. Nevertheless, that could make him an attainable target. Leeds are reportedly open to moving him on, while his injury situation could create an opportunity for a loan agreement with a potential obligation to buy.

West Ham need to be careful with their January recruitment, particularly with promotion within reach. However, Joseph’s age, versatility and previous Championship experience make him an intriguing option. If his recovery progresses as hoped, he could provide valuable competition for Piroe without requiring a significant initial investment.

#2083. Inter-Roma... finisce 6-4. L'analisi in 10 punti della "nostra" corsa scudetto

Non ce ne vogliano Lazio, Milan e Como. Ma oggi, per quel che ha mostrato fino a questo momento la Serie A, la sensazione è che nel momento caldo della stagione la corsa Scudetto possa ridursi a due sole contendenti. Inter e Roma. Per qualità della rosa, gioco espresso, attitudine al successo e interpretazioni dei singoli, ad oggi sono loro due le nostre candidate allo Scudetto con la possibilità di essere smentiti sul campo nelle prossime settimane. Fatta la doverosa premessa, eccoci qua al gioco che ci siamo proposti nella redazione di TuttoMercatoWeb.

- Portieri (ROMA) Pepo Martinez, Provedel e Di Gennaro da una parte. Svilar, Gollini e Vasquez dall'altra. Parte tutto da qui. La sfida a distanza fra Inter e Roma vede passare subito in vantaggio i giallorossi che con Svilar hanno probabilmente il miglior numero uno dell'intero campionato. Dopo la sosta rientrerà, dando un ulteriore boost di qualità alla rosa di Gasperini.

- Difensori (INTER) Il pareggio dell'Inter arriva presto. Qua almeno sulla carta non c'è storia, con i nerazzurri che per qualità ed esperienza ad alti livelli sono sopra ai giallorossi. Con Bisseck, Bastoni, Stones, Akanji, Pavard più il jolly Carlos Augusto, in pochi possono reggere il confronto a livello italiano. Non che la Roma sia messa male con Mancini, Ndicka ed Hermoso più il supporto di Balerdi, Ghilardi e Kourielakis, ma l'Inter vince questo confronto.

- Centrocampisti (INTER) Anche qua primeggia l'Inter. Per centrocampo si intendono i centrali ma pure gli esterni: Dimarco e lo Spence delle ultime uscite sono al top nel ruolo, anche se il confronto con Wesley e Molina non è così impari. Nel mezzo il top della Roma è senza dubbio Manu Kone, l'Inter risponde con i vari Calhanoglu, Barella, Zielinski, Sucic e via dicendo. Nel complesso meglio il reparto di Chivu.

- Attaccanti (INTER) La Roma ha il giocatore che ha segnato di più da inizio 2026 ad oggi. Ma l'Inter ha un pacchetto attaccanti ben più completo. Thuram e Lautaro ovviamente. Più Pio Esposito e Bonny. In Europa poche squadre possono permettersi tanta abbondanza tecnica. La Roma è strutturata diversamente, con Malen cannibale di gol e minuti e Dybala che quando sta bene è fra i migliori della Serie A. Quando sta bene, appunto. Vince l'Inter, ancora.

- Panchina (INTER) Parliamo di profondità della rosa, di possibilità di ruotare i titolari senza abbassare la qualità e di incisività a gara in corso con le sostituzioni. E anche qua ha la meglio Chivu, con scelte top in tutti i reparti, soprattutto a centrocampo. Ma anche le riserve, o presunte tali, di difesa e attacco sono di primissimo livello. Punto su cui alla Roma forse manca qualcosa.

- Allenatore (ROMA) Non ce ne voglia Chivu che ha vinto con grande merito l'ultimo scudetto. Ma il Gasperini che si sta vedendo in questa stagione a Roma sembra avere qualcosa in più, sorretto anche da una spinta motivazionale che potrebbe dare quel quid in più nel momento caldo della stagione.

- Ambiente e tifosi (ROMA) Questo è il punto probabilmente più controverso. O combattuto per meglio dire. L'Olimpico di parte giallorossa continua a macinare sold out ma pure il San Siro nerazzurro è contesto che spinge e regala sostegno. E allora a fare la differenza potrebbero essere le aspettative: perché dall'Inter strafavorita secondo tutti ci si aspetta quasi che debba vincere. Mentre per la Roma, non dovesse arrivare lo Scudetto, non sarebbe certamente un fallimento. E poi c'è l'entusiasmo: col passare delle giornate, quello di fede romanista potrebbe dare quel boost in più alla squadra di Gasperini.

- Società (INTER) Qua vince l'Inter per un ragionamento di struttura e continuità. Il tutto fa riferimento a Beppe Marotta, probabilmente il dirigente migliore che oggi il calcio italiano ha da offrire. La presenza dei Friedkin e il loro coinvolgimento nella Roma sono sempre più forti col passare delle stagioni, ma il comparto dirigenziale dell'Inter (anche con l'addio di Ausilio) resta fra i migliori a livello italiano. E non solo.

- Disponibilità sul mercato di gennaio (ROMA) Qua potrebbe spuntarla la Roma, con il fortissimo coinvolgimento di Gian Piero Gasperini nelle scelte dei rinforzi. Uno, forse due, arriveranno sicuramente. In casa Inter invece valutazioni ancora in corso, ma la possibilità che in inverno non saranno fatti nuovi acquisti esiste. Da qui il vantaggio dei giallorossi.

- Mentalità e abitudine al successo (INTER) Cose che non si possono insegnare e che si apprendono solo col tempo. L'Inter è al vertice in Italia e in Europa da anni, ecco perché è avanti rispetto alla Roma in questo singolo aspetto. Questioni, mentalità e abitudine alla vittoria, che a fine stagione potrebbero avere un peso importante.

- Il peso della Champions Qua le variabili sono tante. E si parte dal capire se Inter e Roma andranno avanti oltre la League Phase, di cui mancano ancora 4 partite. Se una dovesse andare avanti e l'altra no, inevitabilmente anche il campionato potrebbe risentirne. Ma sono discorsi prematuri, nella speranza che entrambe possano superare senza playoff questo primo turno di Champions League.

#2082. Southampton loanee has chance to kickstart his spell after Keven Schlotterbeck injury: Opinion

Southampton had a mixed summer transfer window but one major blow was the sale of Taylor Harwood-Bellis. The Saints brought in multiple defenders in a bid to replace him and one of which was Zach Abbott who joined on loan.

Abbott has a chance to change the course of his Southampton loan spell following Keven Schlotterbeck’s shoulder injury. The Nottingham Forest youngster was introduced during the 1-0 defeat at Preston North End and delivered a composed performance across 79 minutes at centre-back, earning a 6.8 FotMob rating.

It was only his second Championship appearance since arriving on loan in September, but the circumstances have created an opportunity he desperately needs. At 20, Abbott is still developing, and regular first-team football was the obvious attraction of his temporary move to St Mary’s.

Abbott has been honest about his frustration at the lack of minutes, admitting to BBC Radio Solent that he has been “itching for a lot more”. His response to limited opportunities has been to remain patient, work hard in training and take advice from his experienced teammates.

The numbers underline how little football Abbott has played. He has accumulated just 124 Championship minutes across two appearances, completing 74 of his 84 passes at an impressive 88.1% accuracy. He has also won two of his three aerial duels, with his success rate ranking in the 86th percentile among full-backs.

Although the sample size is small, his Preston performance offered encouragement that he can handle the demands of Championship football. His versatility is another advantage, having previously demonstrated an ability to operate at both centre-back and right-back.

Southampton are 7th after 15 games, with 24 points, and remain firmly in the promotion conversation. However, Schlotterbeck’s expected six-week absence leaves Tonda Eckert needing to find a reliable defensive replacement. Nathan Wood and Jack Stephens provide competition, but Abbott has already shown he can step in without looking out of place.

This is an important period for Abbott and both clubs. He joined Southampton to gain the senior experience that was difficult to secure at Nottingham Forest, but his lack of involvement has reportedly raised the possibility of a January recall.

A sustained run of appearances before the new year would change that picture considerably. It would provide Forest with the Championship experience they wanted him to gain and give Southampton another capable defensive option during a crucial period of their campaign.

The upcoming home fixture against Derby County after the international break could be particularly important. Abbott has already reached an FA Cup semi-final and featured in the Europa League, so his potential is clear.

Now he needs regular opportunities to demonstrate it at Championship level. Schlotterbeck’s injury is unfortunate, but it could be the opening Abbott has been waiting for. Southampton should give him the chance to make the most of it.
